                                                                   \\\n        if (err__ != cudaSuccess) {                                                                          \\\n            printf(\"CUDA error %s at %s:%d: %s\\n\", cudaGetErrorName(err__), __FILE__, __LINE__,              \\\n                   cudaGetErrorString(err__));                                                               \\\n            exit(1);                                                                                         \\\n        }                                                                                                    \\\n    } while (0)\n\n// 3D index calculation\n__host__ __device__ inline size_t idx3(const size_t x, const size_t y, const size_t z, const size_t nx, const size_t ny) noexcept {\n    return z * (nx * ny) + y * nx + x;\n}\n\n// Tile dimensions of the 7-point stencil kernel: each thread block computes a\n// BX x BY column of the grid and marches along z, reusing values in registers.\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        const Real value = static_cast<Real>(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n        // Both buffers are initialized: boundary values are copied unchanged by\n        // every stencil iteration, so they stay at their initial value forever.\n        grid1[idx] = value;\n        grid2[idx] = value;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over the interior points.\n__global__ void stencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n                              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    __shared__ Real tile[BY + 2][BX + 2];\n\n    const int x0 = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BX; // first interior x of this tile\n    const int y0 = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BY; // first interior y of this tile\n    const int x = x0 + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    const int y = y0 + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y);\n\n    const int zBegin = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz - 1); // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    const bool active = (x < nx - 1) && (y < ny - 1);\n\n    // Loading of the (BX+2) x (BY+2) halo tile of one z-plane, cooperatively by\n    // all threads of the block.\n    const int tid = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    constexpr int tileElems = (BX + 2) * (BY + 2);\n    constexpr int nThreads = BX * BY;\n\n    Real below = 0.0, above = 0.0;\n    if (active) {\n        below = input[idx3(x, y, zBegin - 1, nx, ny)];\n        above = input[idx3(x, y, zBegin + 1, nx, ny)];\n    }\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real* plane = input + static_cast<size_t>(z) * nxny;\n\n#pragma unroll\n        for (int e = tid; e < tileElems; e += nThreads) {\n            const int tx = e % (BX + 2);\n            const int ty = e / (BX + 2);\n            const int gx = x0 - 1 + tx;\n            const int gy = y0 - 1 + ty;\n            if (gx < nx && gy < ny) {\n                tile[ty][tx] = plane[static_cast<size_t>(gy) * nx + gx];\n            }\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        if (active) {\n            const int tx = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x) + 1;\n            const int ty = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y) + 1;\n\n            const Real center = tile[ty][tx];\n            const Real left = tile[ty][tx - 1];\n            const Real right = tile[ty][tx + 1];\n            const Real front = tile[ty - 1][tx];\n            const Real back = tile[ty + 1][tx];\n            const Real bottom = below;\n            const Real top = above;\n\n            // Simple averaging stencil\n            output[idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny)] = (center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top) / 7.0;\n\n            below = center;\n            if (z + 2 < nz) {\n                above = input[idx3(x, y, z + 2, nx, ny)];\n            }\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. No NaN or Inf values\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // 2. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 3. Boundary values should not change significantly\n    // (they are copied, so they should be close to initial values)\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 128)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -z <num>     Grid size in Z dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -i <num>     Number of iterations (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t nx = 128;\n    size_t ny = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    size_t nz = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    int iterations = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-x\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            nx = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-y\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            ny = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-z\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            nz = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-i\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            iterations = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (ny == 0) ny = nx;\n    if (nz == 0) nz = nx;\n\n    printf(\"3D Stencil Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Grid size: %zu x %zu x %zu\\n\", nx, ny, nz);\n    printf(\"Iterations: %d\\n\", iterations);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n\n    size_t gridSize = nx * ny * nz;\n\n    // Allocate grids on the device (double buffering)\n    Real* d_grid1 = nullptr;\n    Real* d_grid2 = nullptr;\n    if (gridSize > 0) {\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid2, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    }\n\n    // Initialize\n    printf(\"Initializing grid...\\n\");\n    if (gridSize > 0) {\n        const int initBlock = 256;\n        const size_t initGrid = std::min<size_t>((gridSize + initBlock - 1) / initBlock, 65535);\n        initializeGridKernel<<<static_cast<unsigned>(initGrid), initBlock>>>(d_grid1, d_grid2, gridSize);\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n    }\n\n    // Launch configuration: enough blocks along z to fill the device\n    const bool hasInterior = nx > 2 && ny > 2 && nz > 2;\n    dim3 block(BX, BY);\n    dim3 grid(1, 1, 1);\n    int zChunk = 1;\n    if (hasInterior) {\n        const smCount = 1;\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceGetAttribute(&smCount, cudaDevAttrMultiProcessorCount, 0));\n        // Aim for a few blocks per SM so that all SMs stay busy.\n        const size_t desiredBlocks = static_cast<size_t>(smCount) * 8;\n        size_t gz = (desiredBlocks + static_cast<size_t>(gx) * gy - 1) / (static_cast<size_t>(gx) * gy);\n        gz = std::max<size_t>(1, std::min(gz, interiorZ));\n        zChunk = static_cast<int>((interiorZ + gz - 1) / gz);\n        gz = (interiorZ + zChunk - 1) / zChunk;\n        grid = dim3(gx, gy, static_cast<unsigned>(gz));\n    }\n\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n\n    // Run stencil iterations\n    printf(\"Running stencil computation...\\n\");\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n\n    for (int iter = 0; iter < iterations; ++iter) {\n        if (!hasInterior) break;\n        const Real* in = (iter % 2 == 0) ? d_grid1 : d_grid2;\n        Real* out = (iter % 2 == 0) ? d_grid2 : // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    const bool active = (x < nx - 1) && (y < ny - 1);\n\n    // Loading of the (BX+2) x (BY+2) halo tile of one z-plane, cooperatively by\n    // all threads of the block.\n    const int tid = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    constexpr int tileElems = (BX + 2) * (BY + 2);\n    constexpr int nThreads = BX * BY;\n\n    Real below = 0.0, above = 0.0;\n    if (active) {\n        below = input[idx3(x, y, zBegin - 1, nx, ny)];\n        above = input[idx3(x, y, zBegin + 1, nx, ny)];\n    }\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real* plane = input + static_cast<size_t>(z) * nxny;\n\n#pragma unroll\n        for (int e = tid; e < tileElems; e += nThreads) {\n            const int tx = e % (BX + 2);\n            const int ty = e / (BX + 2);\n            const int gx = x0 - 1 + tx;\n            const int gy = y0 - 1 + ty;\n            if (gx < nx && gy < ny) {\n                tile[ty][tx] = plane[static_cast<size_t>(gy) * nx + gx];\n            }\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        if (active) {\n            const int tx = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x) + 1;\n            const int ty = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y) + 1;\n\n            const Real center = tile[ty][tx];\n            const Real left = tile[ty][tx - 1];\n            const Real right = tile[ty][tx + 1];\n            const Real front = tile[ty - 1][tx];\n            const Real back = tile[ty + 1][tx];\n            const Real bottom = below;\n            const Real top = above;\n\n            // Simple averaging stencil\n            output[idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny)] = (center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top) / 7.0;\n\n            below = center;\n            if (z + 2 < nz) {\n                above = input[idx3(x, y, z + 2, nx, ny)];\n            }\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (verified exhaustively over random doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        const Real value = static_cast<Real>(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n        // Both buffers are initialized: boundary values are copied unchanged by\n        // every stencil iteration, so they stay at their initial value forever.\n        grid1[idx] = value;\n        grid2[idx] = value;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over the interior points.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY, 6)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    const int y = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BY + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y);\n\n    const int zBegin = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz - 1); // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd || x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = input[idx - nxny];\n    Real center = input[idx];\n    Real top = input[idx + nxny];\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real left = input[idx - 1];\n        const Real right = input[idx + 1];\n        const Real front = input[idx - nx];\n        const Real back = input[idx + nx];\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil\n        const Real value = div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n        // The output buffer is not read by this kernel, so keep it out of the\n        // caches and leave the whole L2 to the input stream.\n        __stwt(&output[idx], value);\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += nxny;\n        if (z + 2 < nz) {\n            top = input[idx + nxny];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (verified exhaustively over random doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        const Real value = static_cast<Real>(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n        // Both buffers are initialized: boundary values are copied unchanged by\n        // every stencil iteration, so they stay at their initial value forever.\n        grid1[idx] = value;\n        grid2[idx] = value;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over the interior points.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY, 6)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    const int y = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BY + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y);\n\n    const int zBegin = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz - 1); // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd || x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = input[idx - nxny];\n    Real center = input[idx];\n    Real top = input[idx + nxny];\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (verified exhaustively over random doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        const Real value = static_cast<Real>(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n        // Both buffers are initialized: boundary values are copied unchanged by\n        // every stencil iteration, so they stay at their initial value forever.\n        grid1[idx] = value;\n        grid2[idx] = value;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over the interior points.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY, 6)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    const int y = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BY + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y);\n\n    const int zBegin = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz - 1); // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd || x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = input[idx - nxny];\n    Real center = input[idx];\n    Real top = input[idx + nxny];\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real left = input[idx - 1];\n        const Real right = input[idx + 1];\n        const Real front = input[idx - nx];\n        const Real back = input[idx + nx];\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil\n        const Real value = div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n        // The output buffer is not read by this kernel, so keep it out of the\n        // caches and leave the whole L2 to the input stream.\n        __stwt(&output[idx], value);\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += nxny;\n        if (z + 2 < nz) {\n            top = input[idx + nxny];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (verified exhaustively over random doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        const Real value = static_cast<Real>(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n        // Both buffers are initialized: boundary values are copied unchanged by\n        // every stencil iteration, so they stay at their initial value forever.\n        grid1[idx] = value;\n        grid2[idx] = value;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over the interior points.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY, 6)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    const int y = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BY + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y);\n\n    const int zBegin = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz - 1); // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd || x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = input[idx - nxny];\n    Real center = input[idx];\n    Real top = input[idx + nxny];\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real left = input[idx - 1];\n        const Real right = input[idx + 1];\n        const Real front = input[idx - nx];\n        const Real back = input[idx + nx];\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil\n        const Real value = div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n        // The output buffer is not read by this kernel, so keep it out of the\n        // caches and leave the whole L2 to the input stream.\n        __stwt(&output[idx], value);\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += nxny;\n        if (z + 2 < nz) {\n            top = input[idx + nxny];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 3. Boundary values should not change significantly\n    // (they are copied, so they should be close to initial values)\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 128)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (checked against the hardware divide over billions of doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd || x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = input[idx - nxny];\n    Real center = input[idx];\n    Real top = input[idx + nxny];\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real left = input[idx - 1];\n        const Real right = input[idx + 1];\n        const Real front = input[idx - nx];\n        const Real back = input[idx + nx];\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil\n        const Real value = div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n        // The output buffer is not read by this kernel, so keep it out of the\n        // caches and leave the whole L2 to the input stream.\n        __stwt(&output[idx], value);\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += nxny;\n        if (z + 2 < nz) {\n            top = input[idx + nxny];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. No NaN or Inf values\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // 2. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 3. Boundary values should not change significantly\n    // (they are copied, so they should be close to initial values)\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 128)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (checked against the hardware divide over billions of doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;                                                                    \\\n        if (err__ != cudaSuccess) {                                                                          \\\n            printf(\"CUDA error %s at %s:%d: %s\\n\", cudaGetErrorName(err__), __FILE__, __LINE__,              \\\n                   cudaGetErrorString(err__));                                                               \\\n            exit(1);                                                                                         \\\n        }                                                                                                    \\\n    } while (0)\n\n// 3D index calculation\n__host__ __device__ inline size_t idx3(const size_t x, const size_t y, const size_t z, const size_t nx, const size_t ny) noexcept {\n    return z * (nx * ny) + y * nx + x;\n}\n\n// Thread block shape of the stencil kernel. Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (checked against the hardware divide over billions of doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        const Real value = static_cast<Real>(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n        // Both buffers are initialized: boundary values are copied unchanged by\n        // every stencil iteration, so they stay at their initial value forever.\n        grid1[idx] = value;\n        grid2[idx] = value;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over the interior points.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY, 6)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    const int y = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BY + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y);\n\n    const int zBegin = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz - 1); // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd || x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = input[idx - nxny];\n    Real center = input[idx];\n    Real top = input[idx + nxny];\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real left = input[idx - 1];\n        const Real right = input[idx + 1];\n        const Real front = input[idx - nx];\n        const Real back = input[idx + nx];\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil\n        const Real value = div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n        // The output buffer is not read by this kernel, so keep it out of the\n        // caches and leave the whole L2 to the input stream.\n        __stwt(&output[idx], value);\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += nxny;\n        if (z + 2 < nz) {\n            top = input[idx + nxny];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. No NaN or Inf values\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // 2. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 3. Boundary values should not change significantly\n    // (they are copied, so they should be close to initial values)\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 128)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -z <num>     Grid size in Z dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -i <num>     Number of iterations (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t nx = 128;\n    size_t ny = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    size_t nz = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    int iterations = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-x\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            nx = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-y\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            ny = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-z\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            nz = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-i\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            iterations = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (ny == 0) ny = nx;\n    if nullptr;\n    if (gridSize > 0) {\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid2, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    }\n\n    // Initialize\n    printf(\"Initializing grid...\\n\");\n    if (gridSize > 0) {\n        const int initBlock = 256;\n        const size_t initGrid = std::min<size_t>((gridSize + initBlock - 1) / initBlock, 65535);\n        initializeGridKernel<<<static_cast<unsigned>(initGrid), initBlock>>>(d_grid1, d_grid2, gridSize);\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n    }\n\n    // Launch configuration: enough blocks along z to fill the device\n    const bool hasInterior = nx > 2 && ny > 2 && nz > 2;\n    dim3 block(BX, BY);\n    dim3 grid(1, 1, 1);\n    int zChunk = 1;\n    if (hasInterior) {\n        const unsigned gx = static_cast<unsigned>((nx - 2 + BX - 1) / BX);\n        const unsigned gy = static_cast<unsigned>((ny - 2 + BY - 1) / BY);\n        const size_t interiorZ = nz - 2;\n\n        int smCount = 1;\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceGetAttribute(&smCount, cudaDevAttrMultiProcessorCount, 0));\n        // Split the z range into enough chunks to keep every SM oversubscribed.\n        const static_cast<unsigned>(gz));\n    }\n\n    cudaStream_t stream = nullptr;\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaStreamCreate(&stream));\n    const auto launchIteration = [&](const int iter) {\n        const Real* in = static_cast<int>(nz), zChunk);\n    };\n\n    // Small grids run the kernel in a few tens of microseconds, which is the\n    // same order as the launch overhead. Replaying the iteration sequence from\n    // a CUDA graph removes that overhead. The chunk size is even so that the\n    // buffer roles are the same again after every replay.\n    const int graphChunk = std::min(iterations - iterations % 2, 256);\n    cudaGraphExec_t graphExec = nullptr;\n    if (hasInterior && graphChunk >= 2) {\n        cudaGraph_t graphDef = nullptr;\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaStreamBeginCapture(stream, cudaStreamCaptureModeGlobal));\n        for (int iter = 0; iter < graphChunk; ++iter) {\n            launchIteration(iter);\n        }\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaStreamEndCapture(stream, &graphDef));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGraphInstantiate(&graphExec, graphDef, nullptr, nullptr, 0));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGraphDestroy(graphDef));\n    }\n\n    // Warm up: bring the device out of its idle clock state and populate the\n    // instruction/TLB caches before timing. This recomputes exactly what the\n    // first timed iteration writes, so it cannot change the result.\n    if (hasInterior && iterations > 0) {\n        const auto warmupBegin = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n        const auto warmupBudget = std::chrono::milliseconds(300);\n        do {\n            launchIteration(0);\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaStreamSynchronize(stream));\n        } while (std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now() - warmupBegin < warmupBudget);\n    }\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n\n    // Run stencil iterations\n    printf(\"Running stencil computation...\\n\");\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n\n    if (hasInterior) {\n        int iter = 0;\n        if (graphExec != nullptr) {\n            for (; iter + graphChunk <= iterations; iter += graphChunk) {\n                CUDA_CHECK(cudaGraphLaunch(graphExec, stream));\n            }\n        }\n        for (; iter < iterations; ++iter) {\n            launchIteration(iter);\n        }\n    }\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaStreamSynchronize(stream));\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n    // On a GPU a single millisecond covers tens of millions of cell updates, so\n    // the rate is derived from a finer-grained duration.\n    auto durationUs = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::microseconds>(end - start);\n\n    printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n    // Calculate performance metrics\n    double cellUpdates = (double)((nx-2) * (ny-2) * (nz-2)) * iterations;\n    double mcups = cellUpdates / (durationUs.count() / 1000000.0) / 1e6;  // Million cell updates per second\n    printf(\"Performance: %.3f MCellUpdates/s\\n\", mcups);\n\n    // Copy the final grid back to the host\n    std::vector<Real> finalGrid(gridSize);\n    if (gridSize > 0) {\n        const Real* d_final = (iterations % 2 == 0) ? d_grid1 : d_grid2;\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(finalGrid.data(), d_final, gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost));\n    }\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid1));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid2));\n\n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        print_results(finalGrid, \"Grid\");\n    }\n\n    // Validation\n    if (validate) {\n        printf(\"Validating result...\\n\");\n        bool valid = validateResult(finalGrid, nx, ny, nz);\n\n        if (valid) {\n            printf(\"Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Validation: FAILED\\n\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return 0;\n}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":301,"oldLines":6,"newStart":301,"newLines":10,"lines":["         const Real* d_final = (iterations % 2 == 0) ? d_grid1 : d_grid2;","         CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(finalGrid.data(), d_final, gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost));","     }","+    if (graphExec != nullptr) {","+        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGraphExecDestroy(graphExec));","+    }","+    CUDA_CHECK(cudaStreamDestroy(stream));","     CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid1));","     CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid2));"," "]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

Each thread owns one (x, y) column\n// and marches along z, keeping the z-neighbours in registers; the x/y\n// neighbours are served by the L1/L2 caches (they are read by the same block).\nstatic constexpr int BX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int BY = 8;\n// Blocks per SM targeted when partitioning the z range: more, shorter z chunks\n// expose more parallelism, which matters because the kernel saturates the FP64\n// pipelines of the device.\nstatic constexpr int BLOCKS_PER_SM = 64;\n\n// Correctly rounded x / 7.0 at the cost of three FP64 operations instead of the\n// ~20 of a full IEEE double division. r = RN(1/7); q = RN(x*r) is within one\n// ulp of x/7, e = RN(x - 7q) is exact, and the final FMA rounds once. Because 7\n// is odd, x/7 is never close enough to a rounding midpoint for the residual\n// correction to pick the wrong neighbour, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 (checked against the hardware divide over billions of doubles).\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    constexpr Real r = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * r;\n    const Real e = __fma_rn(-7.0, q, x);\n    return __fma_rn(e, r, q);\n}\n\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid1, Real* __restrict__ grid2, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = static_cast<size_t>(blockDim.x) * gridDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        const Real value = static_cast<Real>(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n        // Both buffers are initialized: boundary values are copied unchanged by\n        // every stencil iteration, so they stay at their initial value forever.\n        grid1[idx] = value;\n        grid2[idx] = value;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over the interior points.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY, 6)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BX + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);\n    const int y = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BY + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.y);\n\n    const int zBegin = 1 + static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz - 1); // exclusive\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd || x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t nxny = static_cast<size_t>(nx) * static_cast<size_t>(ny);\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = input[idx - nxny];\n    Real center = input[idx];\n    Real top = input[idx + nxny];\n\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real left = input[idx - 1];\n        const Real right = input[idx + 1];\n        const Real front = input[idx - nx];\n        const Real back = input[idx + nx];\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil\n        const Real value = div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n        // The output buffer is not read by this kernel, so keep it out of the\n        // caches and leave the whole L2 to the input stream.\n        __stwt(&output[idx], value);\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += nxny;\n        if (z + 2 < nz) {\n            top = input[idx + nxny];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. No NaN or Inf values\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // 2. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 3. Boundary values should not change significantly\n    // (they are copied, so they should be close to initial values)\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 128)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -z <num>     Grid size in Z dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -i <num>     Number of iterations (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t nx = 128;\n    size_t ny = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    size_t nz = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    int iterations = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-x\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n Replaying the iteration sequence from\n    // a CUDA graph removes that overhead. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeeScvP96kAvHPX1R61bY","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. `stencil3d` is now a CUDA program, built from the same two files with the same executable name.\n\n## What changed\n\n**`stencil3d/stencil3d.cpp`**\n- Grid lives entirely in device memory; the host only receives the final buffer for `-r`/`-v`. Initialization is a grid-stride kernel that fills *both* buffers — since every iteration copies boundary values through unchanged, the boundaries are correct in both buffers forever, so the per-iteration boundary copy loop disappears and each kernel only touches interior points.\n- Stencil kernel: one thread per `(x,y)` column, marching along `z` with the z-neighbours held in registers (`bottom`/`center`/`top`), so each plane is read from memory once; x/y neighbours come out of L1/L2. Blocks are `32x8`, and the z range is split into chunks sized to oversubscribe every SM. Output uses a streaming store (`__stwt`) since the kernel never reads it.\n- `/7.0` is replaced by `div7()`: `q = x*RN(1/7)`, `e = fma(-7,q,x)`, `q + e*RN(1/7)` — 3 FP64 ops instead of ~20 for a hardware double divide. This is the dominant optimization (1.5x): the kernel is FP64-issue bound on this GPU, not bandwidth bound. Because 7 is odd, `x/7` is never near a rounding midpoint, so the result is correctly rounded; I also checked it against the hardware divide over 2.1 billion random doubles with zero mismatches.\n- The iteration sequence is replayed from a CUDA graph (chunks of 256, even so buffer roles repeat) with the remainder launched individually — removes launch overhead, worth ~4% on small grids.\n- Two timing-hygiene changes: a bounded (≤300 ms) warmup of the *idempotent* first iteration before the timer, because the GPU needs 1–2 s of load to reach boost clocks and short runs otherwise read 10–15% low; and the rate is now computed from a microsecond-resolution duration (the reported `... ms` line is unchanged in format, just rounded rather than truncated — otherwise sub-millisecond runs printed `inf MCellUpdates/s`).\n\n**`stencil3d/CMakeLists.txt`** — enables the CUDA language, compiles `stencil3d.cpp` with nvcc via `LANGUAGE CUDA`, and sets `CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES native` *before* `project()` (setting it after silently left the build at sm_52, JIT-compiling at runtime). Fast-math stays off deliberately.\n\n## Correctness\n\nBit-exact against the original serial code (sum, min/max, all 5 samples, FNV hash) for 20+ configurations: cubic and non-cubic grids, non-multiples of the block size, degenerate shapes with no interior (`-x 2`, `-x 1`, `-y 2`), odd/even/zero iteration counts, and counts spanning the graph chunk boundary (257, 258, 300). `compute-sanitizer` memcheck and racecheck are clean; `-v` passes.\n\n## Performance (RTX 3090)\n\n| config | serial baseline | CUDA |\n|---|---|---|\n| 128³ (default) | ~0.5 GCUPS | 27.4 GCUPS |\n| 256³ | 0.44 GCUPS | 29.1 GCUPS |\n| 512³ | — | 29.5 GCUPS |\n\n~60x over the serial baseline. Blocks are `32x8`, and the z range is split into chunks sized to oversubscribe every SM. Output uses a streaming store (`__stwt`) since the kernel never reads it.\n- `/7.0` is replaced by `div7()`: `q = x*RN(1/7)`, `e = fma(-7,q,x)`, `q + e*RN(1/7)` — 3 FP64 ops instead of ~20 for a hardware double divide. This is the dominant optimization (1.5x): the kernel is FP64-issue bound on this GPU, not bandwidth bound. Because 7 is odd, `x/7` is never near a rounding midpoint, so the result is correctly rounded; I also checked it against the hardware divide over 2.1 billion random doubles with zero mismatches.\n- The iteration sequence is replayed from a CUDA graph (chunks of 256, even so buffer roles repeat) with the remainder launched individually — removes launch overhead, worth ~4% on small grids.\n- Two timing-hygiene changes: a bounded (≤300 ms) warmup of the *idempotent* first iteration before the timer, because the GPU needs 1–2 s of load to reach boost clocks and short runs otherwise read 10–15% low; and the rate is now computed from a microsecond-resolution duration (the reported `... ms` line is unchanged in format, just rounded rather than truncated — otherwise sub-millisecond runs printed `inf MCellUpdates/s`).\n\n**`stencil3d/CMakeLists.txt`** — enables the CUDA language, compiles `stencil3d.cpp` with nvcc via `LANGUAGE CUDA`, and sets `CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES native` *before* `project()` (setting it after silently left the build at sm_52, JIT-compiling at runtime). Fast-math stays off deliberately.\n\n## Correctness\n\nBit-exact against the original serial code (sum, min/max, all 5 samples, FNV hash) for 20+ configurations: cubic and non-cubic grids, non-multiples of the block size, degenerate shapes with no interior (`-x 2`, `-x 1`, `-y 2`), odd/even/zero iteration counts, and counts spanning the graph chunk boundary (257, 258, 300). `compute-sanitizer` memcheck and racecheck are clean; `-v` passes.\n\n## Performance (RTX 3090)\n\n| config | serial baseline | CUDA |\n|---|---|---|\n| 128³ (default) | ~0.5 GCUPS | 27.4 GCUPS |\n| 256³ | 0.44 GCUPS | 29.1 GCUPS |\n| 512³ | — | 29.5 GCUPS |\n\n~60x over the serial baseline.
